4) Public Comment
 Persons may speak for up to three minutes when recognized by the Supervisor. An extension of time of up to five minutes may be
 granted by the Supervisor if the extension will not interfere with the conduct of Board business or with other persons who also
 wish to address the Board.
 Each person wishing to address the Board shall be asked to identify themselves and their address. Persons representing an
 organization or group shall indicate whether their comments represent the official view of the organization or group. Persons
 addressing the Board shall observe rules of common courtesy and decorum.

                      Treasurer’s Report for
                            August 12, 2026

🞇   Cash on Hand (as of 7/31/26) = $1.585M, down $498K from June – Staff maintain
    deposits of township property tax revenues into their respective funds (General, Roads,
    Cemetery and Parks) on a bimonthly basis. No departmental overages. Revenues are 6%
    behind budget and expenses are 3.75% behind due to timing differences as the new FY
    begins.

    Significant payments made include $265K to City for Maple Street and $280K to Progressive
    for the Hall Canopy. We are in fiscal year 2027 currently. Preparing for the fiscal year 2026
    audit for this autumn.

🞇   Tax Year 2026. Summer Tax Bills were mailed out on July 1st. Property Owners can pay
    taxes via ACH without fees. Check the website for the necessary forms to complete.
    Payments are coming in at a steady rate. $436K received to date.
   


🞇   Investments. Total investment value as of 7/31/26 was $7.7M, this is flat to 6/30. Three
    investments will mature in September. Most investments are funds restricted to funds:
    General, Cemetery, Water, Sewer & Roads.

    Treasurer’s Responsibilities. Collecting property taxes for TY2027. Consent Agreement
                                           4 of 212
    includes July Disbursements of $1,166,872.

                                   Saugatuck Township
                              Committee of the Whole Minutes
                             Tuesday, July 7, 2026, at 11:00 a.m.
                                 Saugatuck Township Hall
                                  Meeting held in person

Call to Order: Clerk called the meeting to order at 11:09 a.m.

Roll Call:
Present: Clerk Osman, Treasurer Helmrich, Trustee Marcy, Trustee Aldrich
Absent: Supervisor Bigford
Also Present: Manager DeFranco

Public Comment:
       None

Approval of Agenda:
A Motion was made by Helmrich to approve the agenda as amended. Support by Marcy.
Discussion: DeFranco requested that “Extension of Data Center Moratorium” by added as item
“e” under Discussion Items and that “Saugatuck City Consent Agreement for Fire Station Sewer
Connection” be added as item “f” under Discussion Items.
Motion carried by 4-0 voice vote.

Presentations:
      N/A

Action Items:
         N/A

Discussion Items:
   1. Election Commission Resolution 2026-14
          a. Clerk Osman explained that the Election Commission would be meeting on
              Wednesday to adopt a resolution for Board review.
   2. Approval of 2026 Auditing Services
          a. Board reviewed a proposal from Siegfried Crandal to conduct annual financial
              audit for the Township.
   3. Legal Assistance Center Contract Renewal
          a. Board reviewed renewal of contract with the Allegan County Legal Assistance
              Center to provide support services to Saugatuck Township residents.
   5. Ordinance 2026-04 requiring Tracer Wire for Water Services
          a. Board reviewed a proposed ordinance to require the installation of a tracer wire
              when a water service line is a non-metal material.
   6. Extension of Data Center Moratorium
          a. DeFranco provided an update on the Planning Commission’s drafting of a Data
              Center Ordinance. Explained that an extension to the current Data Center



                                           Page 1 of 3


             Moratorium would be required to provide sufficient time to complete and adopt
             the new ordinance.
   7. Saugatuck City Consent Agreement for Fire Station Sewer Connection
         a. Board reviewed an agreement between the Fire District, Saugatuck City and
             Saugatuck Township to allow the Fire Station to connect to the City’s sewer
             system on Maple Street.


Public Comment:

None.

Board Comments:

N/A

Adjournment: Osman adjourned the meeting at 11:32 p.m.




                                        MOTIONS




                                         Page 2 of 3


   1. A Motion was made by Helmrich to approve the agenda as amended. Support by Marcy.
      Discussion: DeFranco requested that “Extension of Data Center Moratorium” by added
      as item “e” under Discussion Items and that “Saugatuck City Consent Agreement for Fire
      Station Sewer Connection” be added as item “f” under Discussion Items.
      Motion carried by 4-0 voice vote.




Respectfully,

Daniel DeFranco, Township Manager

                                         Saugatuck Township Hall

1) Call to Order/Pledge of Allegiance: Bigford called the meeting to order at 6:00 p.m.

    1) Presentation(s): N/A

    2) Treasurer’s Report: Treasurer Helmrich delivered the report.

    3) Planning Commission Report: Clerk Osman delivered the report

    lead/copper rule, Cemetery almost finalized, Sunset Park and Township Hall.

4) Public Comment:

Heidi Wolff, Attorney running for public office. Name will be on the ballot.

Judge Chris Bernat, appointed to office by the State and will appear on the ballot.

Nate, 134th St – Very distraught with the dust, noise, speed of many trucks delivering and picking up
product from the newly approved facility near the Dog Park. Manager DeFranco will take his concerns to
the Planner Lynee Wells, and a meeting will be scheduled.

Motion by Aldrich, supported by Osman, to approve the Wednesday, July 8, 2026, Saugatuck Township
Board agenda. Motion carried by unanimous voice vote (5-0).

13) Other New Business

   1) Ordinance 2026-04 – Tracer Wire Requirement for Non-Metallic Water Service Lines.

   Ordinance No. 2026-04, an ordinance to amend Section 36-152 of the Township Code of Ordinances
   to require the installation of tracer wire when non-metallic material is used for a water service line
   connected to the Township's water system. The proposed ordinance establishes the tracer wire
   requirement as a condition of connecting to and receiving service from the Township's water
   system. As the owner and operator of the water system, the Township has independent authority to
   establish reasonable requirements governing service connections.

   Motion by Helmrich, supported by Osman, to adopt Ordinance No. 2026-04, an ordinance to amend
   Section 36-152 of the Saugatuck Township Code of Ordinances to require the installation of tracer
   wire when non-metallic water service pipe is used in connection with the Township water system.
   Attached: 1. Ordinance 2026-04 to Amend Section 36-152 of the Saugatuck Township Code of
   Ordinances. Motion carried by unanimous roll call vote (5-0).

                         269.857.7721  3461 Blue Star Hwy  P.O. Box 100  Saugatuck, MI 49453
                                            www.SaugatuckTownshipMI.gov
                                                    17 of2 212


   2) Saugatuck City Consent Agreement for STFD Sewer Connection

   Consent Agreement between Saugatuck Township, the City of Saugatuck, and the Saugatuck
   Township Fire District that would allow the Fire District to connect its Fire Station property to the
   City of Saugatuck's sanitary sewer main on Maple Street. Fire Station property does not have
   frontage on Maple Street and is not a residential lot, the Township's Sewer System Operations
   Contract with the City requires a consent agreement before the connection may be approved. The
   term of the proposed agreement is for a period of thirty (30) years.

   Motion by Aldrich, supported by Marcy, to approve the Consent Agreement for Sanitary Sewer
   Service between the City of Saugatuck, Saugatuck Township, and the Saugatuck Township Fire
   District. Motion carried by unanimous roll call vote (5-0).

14) Future Topics N/A

15) Public Comments: N/A

16) Board Member Comments

Helmrich thanked fellow Commissioners for their support through the past few months. Aldrich was
pleased with his progress. Osman reminded everyone of the upcoming election.

17) Adjournment: Bigford closed the meeting at 6:53 p.m.

Interurban report:


Interurban Update 7/21/2026

Ridership increased by 7.74% from last June, and we carried 833 more passengers than last year. ITA set
another record with a total of 10, 757 passengers. Free Senior Mondays are a hit (62); we carried 172
seniors for free this month. We had 120 destination trips that brought in $596 in revenue. The free parking
shuttle operates on Wednesdays for Music in the Park, and on weekends from 1:00 to 9:00 p.m. Riders can
also use the park-and-call service when the free service isn’t running. We have a regular group of kids who
ride to the Boys 7 Girls Club weekly, sponsored by the Rotary Club. We have scheduled 9 buses and 2
dispatchers for Fridays and Saturdays, with the 2nd dispatcher available to drive if additional buses are
needed. The increase in late-night fare brought in $4,707.00 in revenue.

ITA has three new contracts with the Fennville Elementary Daycare, Fennville Migrant Program, and
Lakeside Learning.

June was a record month for commission on Aging rides (65) for a total revenue of $4,250.00.

Next meeting is Thursday, August 20th, 4:30 pm due to the Transit Conference.

Subject: Riparian Rights

Dear Council Members,

We have lived on Lakeshore Drive, about 1 mile north of M89, for the past 14 years.

There has been construction up and down the road.

Is there some enactment that prohibits homeowners from blocking access to the beach
beyond their property?

We have several cases where the boulders they have put down to protect their bluff reach
all the way into the water meaning people walking on the beach have to stop and turn
around because they cannot continue on the sand.

Does anyone ever check what people are doing to the lake with their out-of-control
revetment projects? Any supervision and inspection of code compliance at all?

Shouldn't we be getting some protection with all the money we pay on our outrageous
taxes?

They are really messing up our lakefront.

Also, RE: the proposed highway replacing Lakeshore Drive. In the paper, one of the
commissioners stated that due to the opposition to this foolhardy and dangerous proposal
that perhaps they should take a "field trip" out there to see what the fuss is about. Please
do so.

Then it will become obvious that our twisty little neighborhood road that crosses ravines
and stream beds and is used daily by dog walkers, joggers, strollers and bikes along with
car traffic, should not be tampered with, especially to the tune of 55 mph!

Adding all that extra width will destroy, mailboxes, gardens, landscaping features, fences,
maybe septic systems, driveways - even the parking lot of the newly restored Sundown
Park, which cost a pretty penny.

So much development in the Township and cities is destroying our natural beauty and the
reasons why people flock to this part of the world.

Disappointedly yours,

Joyce Horath

2554 Lakeshore Drive

FROM: Daniel DeFranco, Township Manager

MEETING DATE: August 12th, 2026

SUBJECT: Resolution of Concurrence for the Housing Tax Increment Financing (TIF) Brownfield Plan

DESCRIPTION:
For the Board's consideration is Resolution No. 2026-16, a Resolution of Concurrence for the Housing Tax
Increment Financing (TIF) Brownfield Plan for the proposed Enclave Project. This is the first Housing
TIF/Brownfield project proposed in Saugatuck Township. Because the Township has not established a
Brownfield Redevelopment Authority (BRA), the Housing TIF Brownfield Plan will be considered and
administered by the Allegan County Brownfield Redevelopment Authority. As part of the County's
approval process, the Township is requested to concur that the Brownfield Plan constitutes a public purpose
and consent to the Housing TIF Brownfield Plan.

The proposed Brownfield Plan has been prepared pursuant to Act 381 and would allow eligible housing
development activities to be reimbursed through tax increment financing. The Plan requests reimbursement
of up to $3,365,000 in eligible housing development activities for the project and includes reimbursement
of up to $600,000 to the Township for construction of a non-motorized path along the south side of Old
Allegan Road between Blue Star Highway and the I-196 overpass. Reimbursement would occur over an
anticipated 16-year period, with 85 percent of the annual tax increment reimbursed to the developer and 15
percent reimbursed to the Township for the pathway project. The Township will continue to receive taxes
based on the property's current taxable value throughout the reimbursement period.

The proposed project advances several long-term Township priorities. The development would add 36 new
for-sale housing units, helping address a housing segment identified by the Allegan County Housing Needs
Assessment as having the greatest unmet demand. The Township's Housing Survey likewise identified a
strong demand for additional housing, with respondents citing limited housing stock as one of the primary
barriers to purchasing a home. In addition, the project includes construction of sanitary sewer infrastructure,
including a new lift station and connection to the regional wastewater treatment system. This investment
will make it possible to extend public sanitary sewer along the Blue Star Highway Corridor from
approximately Maple Street to 134th Avenue, supporting existing and future development in an area
identified by the Township for additional residential growth.

Adoption of the resolution does not approve the proposed development. Rather, it signifies the Township's
concurrence that the Housing TIF Brownfield Plan serves a public purpose and its consent for the Allegan
County Brownfield Redevelopment Authority to consider and administer the Plan. Roll Call.

BUDGET ACTION REQUIRED
N/A

ENGINEER REVIEW
Eligible activities reviewed with Township Engineer

LEGAL REVIEW
Compliance reviewed with Township Attorney

          I. INTRODUCTION
          A. PLAN PURPOSE

          The Allegan County Brownfield Redevelopment Authority (the “Authority”), duly established by resolution
          of the Allegan County Board of Commissioners, pursuant to the Brownfield Redevelopment Financing Act,
          Michigan Public Act 381 of 1996, MCL 125.2651 et. seq., as amended (Act 381), is authorized to exercise
          its powers within the limits of Allegan County, Michigan. The purpose of this Brownfield Plan (the “Plan”),
          to be implemented by the Authority, is to satisfy the requirements of Act 381 for including the eligible
          property described below, designated as The Enclave Project (the “Project”) located on Old Allegan Road,
          east of Blue Star Highway and west of interstate 196 in Saugatuck Township, Allegan County, Michigan
          (the “Property”). The Property consists of one parcel of land that is considered “housing property” as
          defined by Act 381 under Section 2(y). The Property is located within the boundaries of Saugatuck
          Township in Allegan County, the jurisdiction of the Authority.

          This Brownfield Plan will allow the Authority to use tax increment revenue to reimburse the developer,
          Enclave of Saugatuck, LLC (the “Developer”), for the costs of housing development activities required to
          prepare the Property for housing (see Section III).

          B. PROPERTY DESCRIPTION

          The Property consists of one parcel of land located at 6549 Old Allegan Road, Saugatuck Township,
          Allegan County, Michigan. The Property is owned by the Developer and contains approximately 9.7 acres
          of primarily wooded land and is located on Old Allegan Road, east of Blue Star Highway and west of
          interstate 196.

          The general location of the Property is shown on Figure 1. Figure 2 depicts the Parcel Map, which shows
          the eligible property boundary. A concept plan is provided in Appendix A. The Property legal description is
          provided in Appendix B.

          C. BASIS OF ELIGIBILITY

          The Property is eligible for inclusion in this Plan in accordance with MCL 125.2652(p)(ii) because the
          Property is located in the Saugatuck Township in Allegan County, Michigan, and the Property is qualified
          as a “housing property” as defined in Act 381 under Section 2(y). Figure 1 shows a map of the Property
          and Figure 2 depicts the Property parcel eligibility.

          Because the proposed project is located in a community that has identified a specific housing need and
          has absorption data and/or job growth data in the brownfield plan, “housing development activities” as
          defined by Act 381 are considered eligible activities. Descriptions of the project community’s specific
          housing need and job growth data are below.

                   Housing Need: The Allegan County Community Foundation commissioned a Housing Needs
                    Assessment (“HNA”) for Allegan County, which was released by Bowen National Research in
                    2023. The study documents that between 2010 and 2020, the number of households in Allegan
                    County grew 3,527 households or 8.4%, which represents a larger increase compared to the
                    state of Michigan during the same timeframe (4.4%). The increase in the number of households is
                    expected to continue through 2027 with an estimated increase of approximately 1.2% or another
                    565 households. The HNA documents a need of nearly 6,214 housing units by 2027. Of 6,214
                    units needed, there is a gap in for-sale units of approximately 4,329. The Project will facilitate the
                    development of 40 for-sale housing units at price points higher than $379,601, which is affordable
                    households making 121% of the Area Median Income (AMI) or higher. These types of homes
                    represent the largest for-sale gap with an estimated 1,744 units needed by 2027 as shown in the
                    figure below and documented in the HNA on page VIII-8.

© 2026 Soil and Materials Engineers, Inc.
SME and the SME logo are federally registered marks.                                            102275.00_AUG052026_BRP 3
                                                            42 of 212


                   Saugatuck Township recently completed a Housing Survey, attached as Appendix E, which
                    received 515 responses. The majority of respondents were township residents or local business
                    owners. Among respondents, 43 percent reported household incomes of $150,000 or more, while
                    an additional 22 percent reported incomes between $100,000 and $149,999. Of the 515
                    respondents, 52 indicated they were actively seeking a home to purchase or rent within the
                    township. Among those prospective residents, 69 percent expressed interest in a single-family
                    home, while 10 percent sought a townhome or condominium. The primary barriers identified were
                    limited housing inventory and affordability. Additionally, 51 percent of all survey respondents
                    indicated that more single-family housing is needed in the community. These survey results
                    demonstrate a clear demand for additional housing opportunities in Saugatuck Township.
                    Residents, business owners, and prospective residents expressed interest in new housing
                    options across a range of price points and housing types, consistent with the Project proposed by
                    the Developer.
                   Job Growth Data: Between 2013 and 2019, total employment in Allegan County increased by
                    11.3 percent, which represented a larger increase when compared to the State and Nation during
                    the same time period. Since the COVID-19 pandemic, total employment has been increasing
                    incrementally. Since 2022, the total employment within Allegan County has increased by
                    approximately 2.2 percent year over year. This positive trend demonstrates the economy across
                    Allegan County has recovered well post-pandemic. According to the Michigan Department of
                    Technology, Management and Budget, as of February 2026, the unemployment rate in Allegan
                    County was approximately 4.4 percent, which is less than the State of Michigan unemployment
                    rate of 5 percent for the same time period.

          D. PROJECT DESCRIPTION

          The Project will include the construction of 36 site condominiums and will incorporate multiple housing
          types. The architectural style draws from Scandinavian modern design, utilizing standing-seam roofing
          and naturalistic exterior materials. The Project emphasizes low-impact development, mature tree
          preservation, and wetland protection. The Project also includes the construction of a multimodal pathway
          that will extend walking and biking connections to Old Allegan Road and Blue Star Highway for
          households residing on the Project site and surrounding property tenants and owners.

          The total private investment is estimated at $20.2 million. This includes major infrastructure upgrades
          such as a water main extension, sewer service, a new lift station, stormwater facilities, private roadways,
          and site preparation activities, which will support this Project as well as future development along the Old
          Allegan and Blue Star Highway corridor. The anticipated construction timeline is 24-30 months.

          II. GENERAL DEFINITIONS AS USED IN THIS PLAN
          All words or phrases not defined herein shall have the same meaning as such words and phrases
          included in Act 381.

          III. BROWNFIELD PLAN
          A. DESCRIPTION OF COSTS TO BE PAID WITH TAX INCREMENT REVENUES AND
          SUMMARY OF ELIGIBLE ACTIVITIES

          The costs of eligible activities included in, and authorized by, this Plan will be reimbursed with incremental
          local only tax revenues generated by the Property after redevelopment and captured by the Authority,
          subject to any limitations and conditions described in this Plan and the terms of a Development and
          Reimbursement Agreement between the Developer and the Authority (the “Reimbursement Agreement”).
          No personal property taxes are projected to be captured by this Plan at this time, but if incremental
          personal property taxes are generated by this Plan in the future, they will be captured.

          The table below presents the estimated costs of eligible activities for the Project, totaling $4,569,750,
          which qualify for reimbursement.

                                                       TABLE 1 - ELIGIBLE ACTIVITIES
                             MSHDA HOUSING DEVELOPMENT ACTIVITIES                                        COST
           Infrastructure (Developer Funded)                                                          $3,365,000
           Infrastructure (Township Funded)                                                            $600,000
           Brownfield Plan Preparation                                                                  $10,000
           Contingency – 15% (Excludes Brownfield Plan Preparation)                                    $594,750
                                                                 ELIGIBLE ACTIVITIES TOTAL            $4,569,750

          Eligible Activities include:

                   Infrastructure (Developer Funded) – Costs will include the installation of a wastewater lift station
                    and force main, installation of utilities including electric and gas, utility hookups to water and
                    sewer, and the installation of roadways, sidewalks, and public green space. Due to the lack of
                    current infrastructure available to the site, these improvements are necessary for the Developer
                    to complete the housing project.
                   Infrastructure (Township Funded) – Cost includes the installation of a multi-modal pathway
                    adjacent to the Project that will serve as a trail connection to be utilized by residents of the
                    development as well as existing businesses, employees, residents, and any future residents of
                    nearby developments.
                   Brownfield Plan Preparation – Costs related to the preparation and approval of a Brownfield Plan
                    as required by Act 381.

          B. ESTIMATE OF CAPTURED TAXABLE VALUE AND TAX INCREMENT REVENUES

          The 2026 taxable value (“TV”) for real property of the parcel comprised by the Property is reported by the
          Saugatuck Township as $116,943, which is the initial taxable value for this Plan. Projected taxable values
          are included in Table 2. The estimated taxable value based on real property generated by the project
          upon completion is $14,300,000 in tax year 2028 after construction is anticipated to be completed. The
          taxable value is estimated to be approximately 50% of the anticipated value of the homes at completion,
          which is estimated to be $28,600,000. The actual TV will be determined by the actual construction
          completion schedule and the Township Assessor. Incremental personal property will also be captured, if
          available, but is not included in the estimates in this Plan.

          The estimated taxable values, tax increment revenues to be captured, and the impact on taxing
          jurisdictions are presented in Table 2. The actual annual incremental taxable value and captured tax
          increment revenue will be determined by the Township Assessor. The actual increased TV of the land and
          all future taxable improvements on the Property may vary.

          The Plan also accounts for capture for the Authority’s Local Brownfield Revolving Fund (LBRF) in the last
          five years of the Plan.

          C. METHOD OF FINANCING PLAN COSTS AND DESCRIPTION OF ADVANCES BY
          THE MUNICIPALITY

          The Developer is ultimately responsible for funding the costs of eligible activities included in this Plan.
          Neither the Authority nor the Saugatuck Township will advance funding for the eligible activities. All Plan
          funding commitments and activities and cost reimbursements authorized under this Plan shall be
          governed by the Reimbursement Agreement. The inclusion of eligible activities and estimates of costs to
          be reimbursed in this Plan is intended to authorize the Authority to fund such reimbursements. The
          amount and source of any tax increment revenues that will be used for purposes authorized by this Plan,
          and the terms and conditions for such use and upon any reimbursement of the expenses permitted by the
          Plan, will be provided solely under the Reimbursement Agreement.

          Reimbursements under the Reimbursement Agreement shall not exceed the cost of eligible activities and
          reimbursement limits described in this Plan, unless it is amended.

          D. MAXIMUM AMOUNT OF NOTE OR BONDED INDEBTEDNESS

          Not applicable.

          E. DURATION OF BROWNFIELD PLAN

          The duration of the Plan for the project is anticipated to be 16 years. The Developer and Saugatuck
          Township will split the tax increment revenue (TIR) reimbursement for its respective funded portions of the
          Project. The Developer will receive 85% and Saugatuck Township will receive the remaining 15% after the
          Authority receives its administrative capture of 10% on an annual basis. It is estimated that the
          development of the Property will be completed by the end of 2029. As currently proposed, the Developer
          and Saugatuck Township will capture eligible TIR for a period of 16 years.

          F. ESTIMATED IMPACT OF TAX INCREMENT FINANCING ON REVENUES OF
          TAXING JURISDICTIONS

          It is the intent of this Plan for the Authority to only capture available local revenues generated from the
          Project until all incurred eligible brownfield redevelopment costs and Authority administrative costs are
          reimbursed, to the extent described in this Plan. The local tax revenues available for capture for the
          Authority will be based on current millages, which may change as the rates fluctuate over time.

          The estimated taxable values, tax increment revenues to be captured, and the impact on taxing
          jurisdictions are presented in Table 2. The estimated eligible activity reimbursement cash flows are
          presented in Table 3.

          G. LEGAL DESCRIPTION, PROPERTY MAP, PROPERTY CHARACTERISTICS AND
          PERSONAL PROPERTY

          The Property consists of one parcel of land located at 6549 Old Allegan Road, Saugatuck Township,
          Allegan County, Michigan. The Property contains approximately 9.7 acres of primarily wooded land and is
          located on Old Allegan Road, east of Blue Star Highway and west of interstate 196 in Saugatuck
          Township, Allegan County, Michigan.

          The Property and parcel boundaries are shown in Figure 2. The Property and parcel legal description is
          provided in Appendix B. Personal property is not currently included in the capture and reimbursement
          projections because it is unknown to what extent personal property taxes will be generated by the project;
          however, incremental personal property taxes generated by the project will be captured to reduce the
          reimbursement time.

          H. ESTIMATES OF RESIDENTS AND DISPLACEMENT OF FAMILIES

          No persons will be displaced as a result of this development; therefore, a demographic survey and
          information regarding housing in the community are not applicable and are not needed for this Plan.

          I. PLAN FOR RELOCATION OF DISPLACED PERSONS

          No persons will be displaced as a result of this development; therefore, a plan for relocation of displaced
          persons is not applicable and is not needed for this Plan.

          J. PROVISIONS FOR RELOCATION COSTS

          No persons will be displaced as result of this development and no relocation costs will be incurred;
          therefore, provision for relocation costs is not applicable and is not needed for this Plan.

          K. STRATEGY FOR COMPLIANCE WITH MICHIGAN’S RELOCATION ASSISTANCE
          LAW

          No persons will be displaced as result of this development; therefore, no relocation assistance strategy is
          needed for this Plan.

          L. DESCRIPTION OF THE PROPOSED USE OF LOCAL BROWNFIELD REVOLVING
          FUND (LBRF)

          The Authority does not have an established LBRF, therefore, no capture or spending from an LBRF is
          applicable.

          M. OTHER MATERIAL THAT THE AUTHORITY OR GOVERNING BODY CONSIDERS
          PERTINENT

          None.

94         I am wondering whether the township is planning to address short-term rentals (STRs). The
           city of Saugatuck addressed them, and supposedly Douglas is looking to implement caps. If
           so, this will only drive more investment buyers to the township and drive up prices. It's
           undeniable that investment income is driving up the prices and kicking out potential full-
           time members. Lets look at a few examples: 465 S Maple St, listed at $375,000 for 576
           sqft, went pending within 48 hours to be used as a rental. 3296 Blue Star, listed at
           $525,000 for 1,322 sqft. It received a cash offer, sight unseen and went pending within a
           day; sold for $510,000. I wonder what it will be used as? And currently at 3325 Blue Star
           Hwy, we have a 1,104 sqft home for sale for $689,000. The fact that investment buyers are
           in the game is what drives people to list their homes at such outrageous prices. Because
           while that home is listed, a home that is double the size and in a more desirable location at
           6415 Palmetto Ct is listed for sale at $535,000. Palmetto is an HOA subdivision that I
           would venture to guess limits or outright bans STRs. We need some STRs in the township,
           no doubt. But as long as you allow it to be the wild west, especially as neighboring
           communities enact restrictions, we won't be able to get more full-time families in this
           community. The numbers don't lie.

FROM: Daniel DeFranco, Township Manager

MEETING DATE: August 12th, 2026

SUBJECT: 2027 DWSRF Bond Professional Services

DESCRIPTION:

For the Board’s consideration are proposals from Bendzinski & Co. Financial Advisors and Dickinson
Wright to provide the professional services required for the Township's participation in the Michigan
Drinking Water State Revolving Fund (DWSRF) Lead Service Line Replacement Program. EGLE has
released the Fiscal Year 2027 Drinking Water State Revolving Fund Intended Use Plan, and Saugatuck
Township is within the fundable range for lead service line replacement funding. If awarded, the program
would provide a zero-percent interest loan with 10 percent principal forgiveness, allowing the Township to
begin replacing all remaining lead service lines as early as summer 2027.

Participation in the DWSRF requires the issuance of approximately $354,000 in bonds, along with the
engagement of a Registered Municipal Advisor and Bond Counsel. The Township has previously worked
with Bendzinski & Co. Financial Advisors and Dickinson Wright on prior bond issuances and recommends
retaining both firms for the 2027 DWSRF financing.

Bendzinski & Co. has submitted a proposal dated July 10, 2026, to serve as the Township's Registered
Municipal Advisor for a fee of $8,850. In this capacity, Bendzinski & Co. will assist the Township with the
issuance and successful delivery of the DWSRF financing.

Dickinson Wright has submitted a proposal dated July 24, 2026, to serve as Bond Counsel for a fee of
$8,750, plus reimbursement of up to $200 in out-of-pocket expenses. The proposed fee is based on a bond
issuance of $354,000 and would increase by 0.25 percent of any bond proceeds exceeding that amount.

Approval of both proposals is necessary for the Township to proceed with the Drinking Water State
Revolving Fund financing. Voice Vote.

BUDGET ACTION REQUIRED
N/A

ENGINEER REVIEW
Sought in consultation with Township Engineer

LEGAL REVIEW
N/A

Sample Motion:
Motion: Move to approve the proposal from Bendzinski & Co., dated July 10, 2026, to serve as Registered
Municipal Advisor to Saugatuck Township for the issuance of the 2027 Drinking Water State Revolving
Fund bonds.

Motion: Move to approve the proposal dated July 24, 2026, from Dickinson Wright to act as Bond Counsel
for Saugatuck Township in connection with the issuance of the Township's 2027 Drinking Water State
Revolving Fund bonds.

Bendzinski & Co. proposes a fee of $8,850. This fee includes all out-of-pocket expenses, meeting
attendance, mileage, etc. This fee is payable upon closing of the bonds.
        We believe this provides you with the outline of the services we provide. The Registered
Municipal Advisor fee is contingent upon the closing and delivery of the bonds. Although this
form of compensation may be customary, it presents a conflict because Bendzinski & Co. may
have an incentive to recommend unnecessary financings or financings that are disadvantageous
to the Issuer. For example, when facts or circumstances arise that could cause the financing or
other transaction to be delayed or fail to close, Bendzinski & Co. may have an incentive to
discourage a full consideration of such facts and circumstances, or to discourage consideration of
alternatives that may result in the cancellation of the financing or other transaction. Bendzinski
& Co. manages and mitigates this conflict primarily by adherence to the fiduciary duty which it
owes to municipal entities such as the Issuer which require it to put the interests of the Issuer
ahead of its own.
        The Municipal Advisory Council of Michigan (the “MAC”) assesses Bendzinski & Co.,
a $450.00 fee for every bond issue where we act as municipal advisor in the State of Michigan.
This fee will be included in the overall bond costs of issuance. Our membership in the MAC is
voluntary, but the per bond issue assessment is meant to cover costs for credit reports and similar
information available from the MAC that is used in the offering document and in other states is
billed directly by a third-party. The MAC is a single-source municipal database for essential bond
and note details for all local government issuers in Michigan. Among 23 distinctive credit reports,
the MAC is the primary source for Issuer’s debt statements, overlapping debt and indirect debt,
as used to determine suitability and as disclosed in official statements, (if applicable). The MAC
tracks, monitors and records all Michigan new issue bond sales, whether competitive, negotiated
or private placements and bond calls. The MAC does not do any lobbying. Robert J. Bendzinski,
currently serves on the MAC Board of Directors.
         Bendzinski & Co. is registered as a “municipal advisor” pursuant to Section 15B of the
Securities Exchange Act and rules and regulations adopted by the SEC and the MSRB. As part
of this registration Bendzinski & Co. is required to disclose to the SEC information regarding any
criminal actions, regulatory actions, investigations, terminations, judgments, liens, civil judicial
actions, customer complaints, arbitrations and civil litigation involving Bendzinski & Co.
Pursuant to MSRB Rule G-42, Bendzinski & Co. is required to disclose any legal or disciplinary
event that is material to the Issuer’s evaluation of Bendzinski & Co. or the integrity of its
management or advisory personnel. Bendzinski & Co. has determined that no such event exists
as there are no criminal actions, regulatory actions, investigations, terminations, judgments, liens,
civil judicial actions, customer complaints, arbitrations or civil litigation involving Bendzinski &
Co. that were required to be reported to the SEC.
       The MSRB has made available on its website (www.msrb.org) a municipal advisory client
brochure that describes the protections that may be provided by MSRB rules and how to file a
complaint with the appropriate regulatory authority.

       Re:    2026 DWSRF Bonds

Ladies and Gentlemen:

        We look forward to the opportunity to work with the Township of Saugatuck (the “Client"
or “Issuer”) as bond counsel in connection with the issuance of the Client’s 2027 DWSRF bonds
for the purpose of financing improvements to the Client’s water system (the “Project”). The
purpose of this engagement letter ("Agreement") is to describe the services Dickinson Wright
PLLC (the “Firm”) will perform as bond counsel and the Firm’s respective responsibilities and
expectations under this engagement.

Scope of Engagement
       In the Firm’s capacity as bond counsel, the Firm expects to perform the following services:

       (1)     Subject to the completion of proceedings to the Firm’s satisfaction, render the
Firm’s legal opinion (the "Bond Opinion") regarding the validity and binding effect of the Bonds,
the source of payment and security for the Bonds, and the excludability of interest on the Bonds
from gross income for federal and Michigan income tax purposes.

       (2)    Draft the resolutions and closing documents governing the issuance of the Bonds.

       (3)    Prepare and review other documents necessary or appropriate to the authorization,
issuance and delivery of the Bonds, coordinate the authorization and execution of documents, and
review enabling legislation.

       (4)     Prepare the Issuer’s proceedings necessary for the issuance of the Bonds and
prepare all proceedings necessary for approval of the issuance of the Bonds.

       (5)     Assist the Issuer in seeking from other governmental authorities such approvals,
permissions, and exemptions as the Firm determines is necessary or appropriate in connection with
the authorization, issuance, sale, and delivery of the Bonds, except that the Firm will not be
responsible for any required blue-sky filings. The Firm will not be responsible for obtaining any




                                          137 of 212


                                                                       DICKINSON WRIGHT PLLC
Township of Saugatuck
July 24, 2026
Page 2


approvals and permits relating to the construction and operation of the facilities financed with the
proceeds of the Bonds.

       (6)     Review legal issues relating to the structure of the Bonds.

       (7)     Assist the Issuer with all legal issues that arise in connection with the DWSRF
program.

        The Firm’s Bond Opinion will be addressed to the Issuer and will be delivered by us on
the date the Bonds are exchanged for their purchase price (the “Closing”).

        The Firm’s Bond Opinion will be based on facts and law existing as of its date. In rendering
our Bond Opinion, we will rely upon the certified proceedings and other certifications of public
officials and other persons furnished to us without undertaking to verify the same by independent
investigation, and we will assume continuing compliance by the Issuer with applicable laws
relating to the Bonds. During this engagement, we will rely on you to provide us with complete,
accurate, and timely information on all developments pertaining to any aspect of the Bonds and
their security.

        The Firm’s duties in this engagement are limited to those legal services expressly set forth
above, which are services traditionally provided by bond counsel. As attorneys, we do not
represent ourselves as financial advisors or experts and do not provide advice that is primarily
financial in nature, such as advice concerning the financial feasibility of the Project or the
financing, recommending a particular structure for the Bonds as being financially advantageous,
advice estimating or comparing the relative cost to maturity of the Bonds depending on various
interest rate assumptions, or advice regarding the financial aspects of pursuing a competitive sale
versus a negotiated sale.

         Specifically, among other things, our duties under this letter do not include: (a) handling
litigation that may arise with respect to the Bonds; (b) services relating to any grant funds for the
Project or to any contracts or agreements related thereto; (c) preparing requests for tax rulings from
the Internal Revenue Service or no action letters from the Securities and Exchange Commission;
(d) preparing blue sky or investment surveys with respect to the Bonds; (e) making an investigation
or expressing any view as to the creditworthiness of the Issuer or the Bonds; (f) assisting in the
preparation or review of any official statement or other disclosure document with respect to the
Bonds, except as provided in (7) above, or performing an independent investigation to determine
the accuracy, completeness or sufficiency of any such document; (g) advice on post-closing tax
issues (e.g., our engagement does not include rebate calculations for the Bonds); and (h) addressing
any other matter not specifically set forth above that is not required to render our Bond Opinion.

       Attorney-Client Relationship: Upon execution of this engagement letter, the Issuer will
be the client, and an attorney-client relationship will exist between the Issuer and the Firm. The

       In addition, if the Issuer requests us to perform additional services beyond those set forth

upon by the Issuer and the Firm.

        Our representation of the Issuer and the attorney-client relationship created by this

Client's input and approval on any significant decisions or actions that may affect the engagement.

governmental entities, business entities and financial institutions, as well as individuals. It is
possible that, during the time the Firm is representing the Client, some of the Firm's current or
future clients will have disputes or transactions with the Client. The Client agrees that the Firm
may continue to represent or undertake in the future to represent existing or new clients in any
matter, including litigation, even if the interests of such other clients in such other matters are
directly adverse to the Client's, so long as those matters are not substantially related to the Firm's
work for the Client and the Firm's representation of the other clients would not involve the Firm's

Standard Terms of Engagement

        Dickinson Wright PLLC (“the Firm”) is pleased to be retained by the Client to provide legal
services. Below are the standard terms of engagement in relation to any matter on which the
Client retains the Firm, unless otherwise set forth in the Client's engagement letter and subject
always to applicable rules of professional conduct.

    1. Entire Agreement: The engagement letter and these Standard Terms of Engagement
constitute the entire understanding and agreement between the client identified in the
engagement letter (“the Client”) and the Firm regarding the Firm's representation of the Client in
the matter described in the engagement letter. Unless otherwise agreed, they supersede any prior
understandings and agreements, written or oral, and any billing requirements, outside counsel
guidelines, or letters submitted to the Firm. If any provision of the engagement letter or these
Standard Terms of Engagement are held by a court or other arbitrator to be invalid, void, or
unenforceable, the remainder of the provisions shall remain in full force and effect. The Client
should review this document carefully and contact the Firm promptly with any questions. The
Client should retain this document in its file.

    2. The Client: The Firm will provide representation for only the person(s) or entity identified
in the engagement letter. In matters related to corporations, partnerships, and other entities,
unless otherwise agreed in writing, the Firm's representation does not extend to officers, directors,
employees, shareholders, partners, members, or other individuals. Additionally, unless otherwise
agreed in writing, the Firm's representation of an entity does not extend to its affiliates (such as
parent, sister, or subsidiary corporations).

    3. The Scope of our Services: The engagement letter sets forth the specific matter for which
representation will be provided and the scope of the Firm's services. The services the Firm will
provide to the Client may be varied by agreement during the course of the matter. The Firm's
services will not include advice on tax-related issues unless and to the extent specifically
requested by the Client and included in the scope of the Firm's representation.

    At times, the Firm may be called upon to express opinions of law or anticipated outcomes.
Such opinions are limited by the Firm's knowledge of the facts at the time the opinion is rendered,
the present state of the law, and, at times, factors that are unknown or beyond the Firm's control.
Although the Firm will use its best professional judgment, it cannot guarantee the outcome of any
matter.

    4. Primary Attorney: The primary attorney(s) responsible for the Client's relationship with the
Firm may, in the exercise of his/her/their professional judgment, involve other attorneys (including
other members or associates), paralegals, or non-legal professionals possessing special
knowledge or experience to improve efficiency.
    The Firm's invoices for services may reflect time and professional services rendered by
attorneys or other legal personnel associated with the Firm's international or other affiliate(s).
Such attorneys, who are licensed in other jurisdictions, are consulted and serve as legal advisors




                                            142 of 212


                                                                        DICKINSON WRIGHT PLLC
Township of Saugatuck
July 24, 2026
Page 7


to the Firm based on their licensed status in such jurisdictions and expertise in particular legal
specialties.

    5. Basis of Our Charges: Unless other arrangements are made, the Firm's billing for legal
services will be on a per-hour basis. The Firm's standard hourly rates will apply in the absence of
any other agreement, and details of the hourly rates for the attorneys working on the Client's
matter(s) are available upon request. The Firm's hourly rates are subject to periodic reviews and
adjustments, and the Firm reserves the right to revise its hourly rates in accordance with such
general Firm reviews.
       The Firm is often asked to provide estimates regarding the cost of its representation on a
given matter. The Firm is pleased to provide such estimates when, in its professional judgment,
they can be made. Unless the Firm agrees in writing to perform a specific project for a fixed fee,
an estimate will not represent a maximum, minimum, or agreed charge.

    6. File Closure: Upon the completion of the services described in the engagement letter, the
Firm’s representation will be considered concluded. At that time, the Firm will close the file and
retain it in accordance with the Firm’s retention policy.

    7. Records Retention: The Firm acknowledges the importance of client confidentiality, the
protection of personal data, and the need to retain data for legal, accounting, and operational
purposes (including but not limited to personal information, case files, correspondence, and any
other data provided to the Firm in the course of providing legal services). The Firm shall retain
client data for a period not exceeding the duration necessary to serve the purposes for which the
data was collected and processed, including the fulfillment of any legal, regulatory, or ethical
obligations, as well as in alignment with the Firm’s retention policies. Data shall be maintained in
a secure environment with appropriate safeguards against unauthorized access, alteration, or
destruction and in compliance with applicable data protection laws. Upon the expiration of the
retention period the Firm shall securely destroy the data in a manner that is consistent with best
practices for the protection of confidential information and the environment. Client consents to
the destruction of the file upon the expiration of the retention period.

     8. Retainers: Unless otherwise set forth in the engagement letter, it is understood that the
Firm may withdraw amounts from the retainer at any time as may be necessary to satisfy
outstanding invoices. If at any time the retainer proves insufficient to cover past due invoices or
falls below the agreed amount, the Firm may require that it be replenished.

    9. Conflicts of Interest: Conflicts of interest are a concern for the Firm and the clients it
represents. The Firm attempts to identify actual and potential conflicts at the outset of any
engagement and may request that the Client sign a conflict waiver before the Firm accepts an
engagement from the Client. Occasionally, other clients or prospective clients may ask the Firm
to seek a conflict waiver from the Client so that the Firm can accept an engagement on their
behalf. Please do not take such a request to mean that the Firm will represent the Client less
zealously; rather, it indicates that the Firm takes its professional responsibilities to all clients and
prospective clients very seriously.

Township of Saugatuck
July 24, 2026
Page 8


   Unfortunately, conflicts sometimes arise or become apparent after work begins on an
engagement. When that happens, the Firm will do its best to address and resolve the situation in
a manner that is consistent with its professional responsibilities.
    The Firm will not represent any other client on any matter on which the Firm is representing
the Client unless the Firm has the Client's express agreement that it may do so and where
permitted to do so by the applicable jurisdiction's Rules of Professional Conduct.
   Client agrees that the Firm may also act generally for another client which, for the Client, is a
market competitor.

    10. Liability Insurance Coverage: It is the Client's responsibility to ascertain whether the
Client is covered by any relevant insurance in respect of either liability or legal expenses. If so,
the Client is responsible to notify the Client's insurer(s) of the claim or potential claim and the
Firm's involvement as soon as possible. It is also the Client's responsibility to inform the Firm if
the Client believes that the Client has insurance coverage for the specific matter for which the
Firm has been retained.

    11. Termination of Representation: The Client may terminate the Firm's representation at
any time, with or without reason. The Firm has a right to discontinue providing services under
certain circumstances, such as the Client's failure to fulfill financial obligations to the Firm. The
Client's termination of the Firm's representation in no way relieves the Client of the obligation to
pay for legal services that have been provided prior to the time of termination and that are
necessitated to make an orderly transfer of the Firm's file materials.
   Upon termination of the Firm's representation for any reason, the Firm will return the Client's
papers, documents, and other property to the Client upon receipt of the Client's request for them.
The Firm may, and likely will, retain a copy of the materials returned to the Client. If the Client has
outstanding invoices owing to the Firm, the Firm may have the right to retain the Client's
documents if they are properly subject to a lien.
    At such time as the Firm has completed the scope of work for which the Firm has been
retained, the Firm will consider its representation to have ended. If the Client later retains the Firm
to perform further or additional work, the Firm's future representation will be subject to the terms
and understanding set forth herein, unless other terms and conditions are expressly agreed to.
   Furthermore, upon termination of the Firm's representation, any and all outstanding legal fees
and costs incurred by the Firm for its legal services rendered to the Client in connection with the
engagement will become immediately due and owing. In the event the Client fails to immediately
pay any outstanding legal fees and costs owed to the Firm, the Firm reserves all rights and
remedies available to it for collection of any and all amounts of money owed to it for said legal
services. The Client also agrees to pay all charges, costs, expenses, and reasonable attorney's
fees incurred by the Firm in enforcing and recovering any and all legal fees and costs incurred
pursuant to the engagement letter.

    12. E-mail and Cellular Phone Authorization: The Firm is able to communicate with clients
via electronic mail over the internet ("e-mail"), and many of the Firm's attorneys utilize cellular
phones. With e-mail, current technology cannot eliminate the risk that confidences and/or secrets
otherwise protected by attorney/client privilege may be viewed by unauthorized third parties and

being transmitted. The Client retains the right to direct Dickinson Wright PLLC not to send specific
items of information via the internet, by e-mail, or over a cellular phone. This authorization shall

    13. Post-Engagement Matters: The Client is engaging the Firm to provide legal services in
connection with a specific matter. After completion of the matter, changes may occur in the
applicable laws or regulations that could impact the Client's future rights and liabilities. Unless the
Client engages the Firm after the completion of the matter to provide additional legal advice on
issues arising from the matter, the Firm has no continuing obligation to advise the company on
such issues or on future legal developments, including monitoring renewal or notice dates or
similar deadlines that may arise with respect to the matter.

    14. Privacy: The Firm recognizes the importance of data privacy and is committed to
protecting the confidentiality, integrity, and availability of all personal and business information in
compliance with all applicable data protection laws and regulations. The Firm will only collect
personal and business information that is necessary for the fulfillment of its duties and within the
scope of its services. The information collected shall be used exclusively for the purposes for
which it was provided and other compatible purposes unless the Client provides explicit consent
to the contrary or where it is required or permitted by law. For inquiries, or to remove personal
data from the firm’s systems upon completion of the engagement, please contact the firm directly.

     15. Corporate Transparency Act (CTA) Disclaimer: Under the Corporate Transparency Act
("CTA"), certain entities organized in the U.S. (including entities that are disregarded for federal
income tax purposes) and foreign entities doing business in the U.S. are required to report
information to the Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) as to their beneficial
ownership. The report must provide information regarding the entity, each beneficial owner, and
(in some cases) each company applicant. Entities subject to beneficial ownership information
(BOI) reporting include corporations, limited liability companies, and any other entity created by
filing a document with the secretary of state or similar office under state, Tribal, or foreign law.

FROM: Daniel DeFranco, Township Manager

MEETING DATE: August 12th, 2026

SUBJECT: Extension of Temporary Moratorium on Data Centers

DESCRIPTION:

For the Board’s consideration is a three-month extension of the temporary moratorium on the acceptance of
new building permit and zoning applications for data center development. In February 2026, the Township
Board adopted a six-month temporary moratorium to provide the Township with time to evaluate
appropriate regulations for data center development. The existing moratorium is scheduled to expire at the
end of this month.

Since the moratorium was adopted, Township staff has prepared a comprehensive draft ordinance
regulating data center development. The Township intends to hold a public hearing on the proposed
ordinance before the Planning Commission in August 2026. The proposed three-month extension would
provide sufficient time for the Planning Commission to complete its review and make a recommendation to
the Township Board for consideration and approval.

Staff recommends that the Township Board adopt Resolution 2026-17 extending the existing moratorium
for an additional three months. Roll Call.

BUDGET ACTION REQUIRED
N/A

COMMITTEE/COMMISSION REVIEW
N/A

LEGAL REVIEW
Resolution prepared by Township Attorney

Sample Motion:
“Motion to adopt Resolution 2026-17, A Resolution to Extend the Existing Moratorium on the Acceptance
of Building and Zoning Applications for Data Center Development, Pending the Approval of a New
Ordinance.”

    1. Resolution 2026-17




                                         147 of 212


                                          TOWNSHIP BOARD
                                       SAUGATUCK TOWNSHIP
                                        Allegan County, Michigan

Board member ____________________, supported by Board member ____________________, moved
the adoption of the following resolution:

                                          RESOLUTION 2026-17
        A RESOLUTION TO EXTEND THE EXISTING MORATORIUM ON THE
        ACCEPTANCE OF BUILDING AND ZONING APPLICATIONS FOR DATA
        CENTER DEVELOPMENT, PENDING THE APPROVAL OF A NEW
        ORDINANCE
WHEREAS, in February, 2026, the Township Board adopted Ordinance No. 2026-01, which imposed a 6-
month moratorium on the acceptance of building and zoning applications for data center development
within the Township, in order to allow sufficient time for the development and consideration of a new
ordinance regulating such uses (the “Moratorium Ordinance”); and
WHEREAS, the moratorium took effect upon publication of the Moratorium Ordinance and, by its terms,
expires six (6) months from the date of publication; and
WHEREAS, since the moratorium has been in effect, Township staff has prepared a comprehensive draft
ordinance regulating data center development, and the Township intends to hold a public hearing on the
proposed ordinance in August 2026; and
WHEREAS, the Township Board wishes to extend the moratorium, as permitted by Section 4 of the
Moratorium Ordinance, to allow sufficient time for the proposed ordinance to be considered, approved, and
take effect before the moratorium expires;

NOW, THEREFORE, the Township Board resolves as follows:
A. The moratorium is hereby extended through the earlier of the following: (i) 11:59 p.m. on October 31,
2026; or (ii) the effective date of a new ordinance regulating data center development adopted by the
Township Board.
B. All resolutions and parts of resolutions are, to the extent of any conflict with this resolution, rescinded.

Yeas: Council member:
Nays: Council member:
Abstain: Council member:
Absent: Council member:

RESOLUTION DECLARED ADOPTED.

FROM: Daniel DeFranco, Township Manager

MEETING DATE: August 12th, 2026

SUBJECT: Fire Safety Waiver and Indemnity Restrictive Covenant Agreement

DESCRIPTION:

For the Board's consideration is a Fire Safety Waiver and Indemnity Restrictive Covenant Agreement
between Saugatuck Township, the Saugatuck Township Fire District, and Riverside House, LLC. The
property owner has installed heated tubing within and beneath the driveway and has requested that the Fire
District waive the International Fire Code site plan condition requiring physical testing of the completed
driveway with fire apparatus in order to avoid potential damage to the system.

In exchange for the waiver, the property owner agrees to defend, indemnify, and hold harmless the
Saugatuck Township Fire District and Saugatuck Township from claims arising out of damage to the
property caused by emergency response activities, as well as claims related to injuries or property damage
attributable to delayed emergency response resulting from the waived testing requirement. The agreement
was prepared by the Fire District Attorney, reviewed by the Township Attorney, and has been executed by
the property owner. Voice Vote.

BUDGET ACTION REQUIRED
N/A

COMMITTEE/COMMISSION REVIEW
N/A

FROM: Daniel DeFranco, Township Manager

MEETING DATE: August 12th, 2026

SUBJECT: Consent Agreement for Sanitary Sewer Service

DESCRIPTION:

For the Board’s consideration is a Consent Agreement for Sanitary Sewer Service between Saugatuck
Township, the City of Saugatuck, and the Saugatuck Township Fire District. The Fire District is currently
served by a private septic system and desires to connect to the public sanitary sewer system. While the Fire
Station is located in Saugatuck Township on Blue Star Highway, public sanitary sewer is only available
within the City of Saugatuck on Maple Street. Under the existing Sewer Operations Agreement between the
Township and the City, Township parcels not located on Maple Street require a Consent Agreement to
connect to the City's sanitary sewer system.

The proposed agreement authorizes the Fire District to connect to the Maple Street sanitary sewer system
within 36 months. Sewer flow from the Fire Station will count against the City of Saugatuck's allocated
treatment plant capacity based on a baseline average monthly water usage of 20,000 gallons. If average
monthly water usage exceeds 115 percent of the baseline, the excess flow will instead count against
Saugatuck Township's allocated capacity in the wastewater treatment plant.

Approval of the Consent Agreement will allow the Fire District to proceed with the sanitary sewer
connection while maintaining consistency with the existing Sewer Operations Agreement and clearly
defining how treatment plant capacity will be allocated. The Agreement has been reviewed and approved
by the Saugatuck City Council. Voice Vote

BUDGET ACTION REQUIRED
N/A

ENGINEER REVIEW
Reviewed by Township Engineer

LEGAL REVIEW
Reviewed by Township Attorney

Sample Motion:
“Motion to approve the Consent Agreement for Sanitary Sewer Service between Saugatuck Township, the
City of Saugatuck, and the Saugatuck Township Fire District.”

FROM: Daniel DeFranco, Township Manager

MEETING DATE: August 12th, 2026

SUBJECT: Sewer Lateral Cost Reimbursement Agreement

DESCRIPTION:

For the Board's consideration are two agreements for the installation of sanitary sewer laterals in
conjunction with the City of Saugatuck's Maple Street reconstruction project. With the road reconstruction
underway, there is an opportunity to install the laterals before Maple Street is repaved, avoiding the need to
disturb the newly improved roadway for future sewer connections. Under both agreements, the Township
would initially pay the City for the cost of the lateral installation and be reimbursed by the respective
property owners.

The first is a Cost Reimbursement and Lien Agreement for a sanitary sewer lateral to serve Parcel No. 03-
20-010-029-10, located on Blue Star Highway. While an existing utility easement provides access between
the property and Maple Street, there is currently no sewer lateral available to serve the property. At the
Township's request, the City has agreed to add installation of a six-inch sewer lateral to its existing
construction contract and bill the Township directly for the work. The cost is currently estimated not to
exceed $5,000, with the final amount based on the City's actual cost. The agreement is contingent upon the
City and KLSWA providing written approval for the requested six-inch sewer capacity before the Township
incurs any cost.

Under the agreement, Steven Voss and Saugatuck Villas, LLC agree to reimburse the Township for the full
cost of the sewer lateral within 30 days of receiving an invoice and supporting documentation from the
Township. Any unpaid amount will accrue interest at 6.5 percent and be secured by a lien against the
property. The agreement applies only to the cost of installing the lateral and does not waive or otherwise
affect any other applicable sewer fees or charges.

The second is a Special Assessment Agreement with Tyler and Renee Starow for their property at 211 N.
Maple Street. Under the agreement, the Township would pay the cost of installing the sewer lateral as part
of the Maple Street reconstruction project. The property owners would reimburse the Township through a
special assessment paid over a five-year period at 6 percent interest.

Approval of the agreements will allow both sewer laterals to be installed in coordination with the Maple
Street reconstruction while providing for reimbursement of the Township's costs by the benefiting property
owners. Voice Vote

BUDGET ACTION REQUIRED
N/A

ENGINEER REVIEW
Reviewed by Township Engineer

                    COST REIMBURSEMENT AND LIEN AGREEMENT
              (SEWER LATERAL INSTALLATION — MAPLE STREET PROJECT)
This Cost Reimbursement and Lien Agreement (this “Agreement”) is made as of _______________, 2026,
between the Township of Saugatuck, a Michigan general law township with a mailing address of 3461 Blue
Star Highway, Saugatuck, MI 49453 (the “Township”), and Steven Voss, an individual, whose address is
5483 W. Melinda Lane, Glendale, AZ 85308 (the “Owner”), and Saugatuck Villas, LLC, a Michigan limited
liability company, whose address is 31 Butler Street, Box 316, Saugatuck, MI 49453 (the “Co-Obligor”).
                                                RECITALS
A. The Owner owns the property commonly known as V/L Blue Star Highway, Saugatuck, Michigan
(Parcel No. 03-20-010-029-10), and more particularly described on the attached Exhibit A (the “Property”).
The Co-Obligor is the developer of the improvements on the Property.
B. The City of Saugatuck (the “City”) is undertaking a road improvement project on Maple Street (the
“Project”).
C. In conjunction with the Project, a six-inch (6") sewer lateral is to be installed to serve the Property,
extending to the east right-of-way line of Maple Street (the “Lateral Work”).
D. At the request of the Township, the City has agreed to add the Lateral Work to its existing Project
contract and to bill the Township directly for the cost of the Lateral Work attributable to the Property.
E. The Owner and the Co-Obligor desire that the Lateral Work be installed as part of the Project, and the
Township is willing to advance the cost of the Lateral Work to the City on the condition that the Owner
and the Co-Obligor reimburse the Township for that cost on the terms set forth below.
F. The parties are entering into this Agreement to establish the obligation of the Owner and the Co-Obligor
to reimburse the Township for the cost of the Lateral Work, to secure that obligation by a lien upon the
Property, and to set forth the parties’ related rights and obligations, subject to the following terms and
conditions.
G. The Owner and the Co-Obligor are requesting approval from the City and the Kalamazoo Lake Sewer
and Water Authority (“KLSWA”) to connect to the sewer with a six-inch (6") line from the Project to the
Maple Street sewer.
                                      TERMS AND CONDITIONS
In exchange for the consideration in and referred to by this Agreement, the parties agree as follows:
1. Reimbursement Obligation. The Owner and the Co-Obligor jointly and severally agree to reimburse
the Township for the full cost of the Lateral Work attributable to the Property, as billed to the Township by
the City (the “Reimbursement Amount”). The parties acknowledge that the Reimbursement Amount is
presently estimated not to exceed $5,000.00, and that the final Reimbursement Amount shall be the actual




                                               167 of 212


amount billed to the Township by the City for the Lateral Work attributable to the Property. The Township
shall provide the Owner and the Co-Obligor with documentation of the amount billed by the City. The
obligations of the parties under this Agreement are each conditioned upon the City’s and KLSWA’s written
approval of the request for six-inch (6") sewer capacity for the Project, and no such obligations shall arise
unless and until that written approval is issued prior to the Township incurring any cost for the Lateral
Work.
2. Payment. The Owner and the Co-Obligor shall, jointly and severally, pay the Reimbursement Amount
to the Township in full within thirty (30) days after the Township delivers to them an invoice for the
Reimbursement Amount together with the supporting documentation described in Section 1 (the “Due
Date”). Any amount not paid by the Due Date shall accrue interest at the rate of 6.5% per annum until paid.
3. Lien. Any amounts due under this Agreement shall be a lien upon the Property collectible in the same
manner as liens for real property taxes, pursuant to the General Property Tax Act, Act 206 of 1893, as
amended. This lien shall attach as of the date this Agreement is recorded and shall remain in effect until the
Reimbursement Amount and all accrued interest are paid in full.
4. Scope. This Agreement pertains to the Lateral Work only. It does not defer, waive, or otherwise affect
the Owner’s obligation to pay any other applicable fees, charges, or assessments relating to the Property or
to the provision of water and sewer service to the Property.
5. Recording and Run with the Land. The Township shall record this Agreement in the office of the
Allegan County Register of Deeds only if the Reimbursement Amount, or any portion thereof, becomes
past due. Upon such recording, this Agreement shall run with the land as follows:
         a.      If the Owner conveys the Property to a third party (the “Grantee”) before paying the total
    amount owed under this Agreement, the Owner and the Grantee shall be jointly and severally liable for
    that amount until such liability is transferred pursuant to this paragraph. The Grantee may fully assume
    the Owner’s liability with the written approval of the Township Manager or his or her designee, which
    shall be freely granted unless the Township Manager or his or her designee finds good cause to believe
    the Grantee is unable to pay.
         b.      If the Owner conveys an interest in any portion of the Property to a Grantee before paying
    the total amount owed under this Agreement, the Owner shall remain solely liable for that amount until
    such liability is transferred pursuant to this paragraph. The Grantee may assume a portion of the
    Owner’s liability, determined based on a proportionate share of the Property being acquired, by written
    agreement signed by the Township, the Owner, and the Grantee.
6. Entire Agreement. This is the entire Agreement between the parties as to its subject matter. It supersedes
any prior or contemporaneous agreements. It may not be modified except in writing, signed by both parties.
7. Dispute Resolution. In case of any dispute pursuant to this Agreement, the parties agree that, to the
extent not otherwise prohibited by law, the jurisdiction and venue for any such dispute shall be solely within
the state courts located in Allegan County, Michigan. The parties further agree that in any such dispute the
prevailing party shall, in addition to any other relief to which it may be entitled, be awarded its actual cost,
including, without limitation, filing fees, discovery costs, actual reasonable attorneys’ fees, expert witness
fees, and other costs incurred to bring, maintain, or defend any such action from its first accrual or notice
thereof through all appellate and collection proceedings.
The parties have signed this Agreement on the dates stated below.
